# HMTc Category 22 (Home Air and Inhalation-Adjacent Products) — standard derivation report
Method: HMTc Method v2.0 — government-limit default. Every cell is the strictest applicable sovereign
maximum for this exact product form in its native basis, a disclosed read-across where the exact cell
is unregulated, a substantiated not-material determination, or a control. No cell is occurrence-derived.
CATEGORY-SPECIFIC METHOD NOTES. (1) INHALATION IS THE DEFINING PATHWAY AND THE PANEL IS STILL A CONTENT SCREEN. Every row here is used by aerosolisation, evaporation or combustion and the exposure of concern is respiratory. A limit expressed per kilogram of product is NOT an inhalation exposure limit and is not presented as one. HMTc publishes no emission-rate limit, no per-actuation dose limit and no indoor-air concentration limit for this category, because no credible sovereign government sets one for any of these product forms. What the panel governs is the content of the material that is put into the air, plus the components in the emission path. No occupational exposure limit and no ambient- or indoor-air guideline is converted into a product ceiling anywhere in this category. (2) THE READ-ACROSS IS TAKEN ON THE ROUTE AXIS, AND IT IS DISCLOSED. No government anywhere sets a heavy-metal content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The nearest applicable sovereign cells are the route-scoped content maxima that US state cannabis regulators set for products administered by INHALATION, and they are nearest not by matrix but by route and by basis. Colorado's 2022 table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 each publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices - inhalation strictest, then oral, then cutaneous - which demonstrates that the value is a function of the route of administration rather than of the source matrix's chemistry. Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray". Maryland's route gap is the sharpest evidence available: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg, a factor of about 1,833. Every such cell is published as `read-across` with the source instrument named, never as `regulatory-alignment`. (3) WHAT THE READ-ACROSS DOES NOT DO. It does not re-derive an exposure assessment. Consumption mass differs between a cannabis product and a candle, and HMTc does not adjust the sovereign value up or down to account for that: Method v2.0 forbids an occurrence-derived, percentile, feasibility or precautionary adjustment in either direction. The adopted values are three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material; the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal; REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg), so the read-across tightens the applicable regulatory position rather than relaxing it. (4) THE CANDLEWICK PROHIBITION IS A PROHIBITION, NOT A CEILING. 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13) declares candles with lead-cored wicks, and such wicks in themselves, to be banned hazardous substances, using 0.06 percent lead by weight of the metal in the wick core as the definitional threshold. Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956 mirror it. That threshold is measured on the metal of the wick core, in a basis that is not any row's basis here, and it is NOT published as this category's lead ceiling: publishing 600,000 ug/kg against a scented candle would misrepresent a prohibition as a permission. It is carried on the scented-candles row as an independent pass/fail component hard stop instead. The wickless wax-melt row does NOT carry it, because a melt has no wick of its own and the heat source is a separate product; that limitation is stated rather than papered over. (5) COMBUSTION AND EVAPORATION CONCENTRATE, THEY DO NOT DESTROY. Burning a candle or an incense stick, and heating an oil reservoir, remove volatile mass and leave the non-volatile metal behind, partitioned between the emitted soot and aerosol and the residual wax, ash and reservoir film. A content screen on the unburnt article is therefore a LOWER BOUND on the metal available to the emission path, not a measure of what is emitted. Combustion and heated rows carry mandatory post-burn residue, ash or reservoir-film analysis in their reference methods for exactly that reason, and hexavalent chromium is speciated on the residue as well as on the article because flame and glow temperatures oxidise trivalent chromium. (6) FIVE PANEL CELLS HAVE NO SOVEREIGN NUMBER ANYWHERE AND ARE HELD UNDER CONTROL. No inhaled-product instrument in any jurisdiction publishes nickel, tin, aluminium, inorganic arsenic or hexavalent chromium. Each of those cells is a control with a named reference method, a component-level sampling plan and an escalation rule. None is declared not-material, because each is genuinely material in at least part of this category, and none is given an invented ceiling. (7) SPECIES NEVER REPLACE TOTALS. Inorganic arsenic does not satisfy total arsenic, methylmercury does not satisfy total mercury, and hexavalent chromium does not satisfy total chromium. Total chromium is published as a SCREEN and is excluded from the firm-limit count; hexavalent chromium carries a zero-tolerance disposition rather than a number. (8) METHYLMERCURY IS SPLIT BY FORMULATION CLASS, NOT ASSUMED. Twelve rows whose emitting material is a manufactured non-marine formulation carry a not-material determination bounded by total mercury. Three rows do not: incense sticks and cones, potpourri and sachets, and the residual row, because those admit marine-derived and wild-harvested animal material (ambergris and marine-animal perfumery fixatives, dried seaweed, shell and coral decorative elements, undetermined natural inputs) and a categorical no-methylation-pathway claim would be false for part of the row. Those three are controls. (9) REGISTER GAPS ARE DISCLOSED, NOT FILLED. The Australian candle-wick lead rows are present on the register (au_customs_1956_pb_candles_wicks_0_06pct, au_customs_1956_pb_candle_wicks_0_06pct) but jurisdiction AU sits outside both the default credible set and this category's widening, so they are disclosed in prose and not named as anchors. Whether the Colorado and California cannabis instruments publish nickel, tin or aluminium values that this register simply lacks is unverified and is recorded as a register gap rather than assumed either way. The RIVM air-freshener product-type list and the EU CLP Regulation, both named as regulatory anchors in the taxonomy entry for this category, set no heavy-metal content maxima: RIVM supplies an exposure-factor framework and CLP classifies hazards, so neither yields a number and neither is on the register. Nothing in this config is written from memory; every number resolves to a named register row.
Native basis family: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration.
Credible-regulator set: EU, UK, CODEX, international, US, AU-NZ, CA, HK, US-CA, US-CO, US-MD, US-MA, US-NV, IL.
Credible-set note: Method v2.0 requires the strictest applicable maximum set by a CREDIBLE SOVEREIGN GOVERNMENT. For products administered by inhalation the only governments that have set heavy-metal content maxima at all are US state cannabis regulators and the Israeli Ministry of Health medical-cannabis unit; the US federal government sets none, because federal law prohibits the underlying commodity rather than regulating it, which makes the states the only sovereign regulator on the subject. The credible set is therefore widened for this category to admit US-CA (California Department of Cannabis Control), US-CO (Colorado Marijuana Enforcement Division), US-MD (Maryland Cannabis Administration), US-MA (Massachusetts Cannabis Control Commission), US-NV (Nevada Cannabis Compliance Board) and IL (Israel Ministry of Health). This is the same governance ruling already made for HMTc Category 25 (Cannabis) and it is recorded here rather than assumed. The widening is confined to route-scoped inhaled-product instruments: the audit scan reaches only register rows whose product scope is written in route terms, and the non-route-scoped instruments from these jurisdictions (the Massachusetts all-uses protocol, the Nevada unspeciated action levels, the Israeli IMC-GMP Procedure 152 table) are dispositioned explicitly in prose in every numeric cell's substantiation rather than silently ignored. No dietary, drinking-water, cosmetic, jewellery, toy, fertiliser or biosolids row from any jurisdiction anchors a cell in this category, and the basis guard on every row enforces that mechanically.
Provenance tags: regulatory-alignment | read-across | not-material | control | ratchet.

Version: cat22-v1.0 (2026-08-17)
### instant-air-refreshment-sprays — Instant air refreshment sprays (aerosol cans, trigger sprays)
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the filled aerosol formulation as dispensed (fragrance concentrate, solvent and carrier phase, and liquefied or compressed propellant), presented in a tinplate or aluminium can with a metering or continuous valve
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the fragrance concentrate; the solvent and carrier phase (ethanol, water, glycols); the liquefied or compressed propellant; the tinplate or aluminium aerosol can and its internal lacquer; the valve cup, stem, gasket and dip tube; the actuator and any nickel-plated or chrome-plated trim; and any added colorant. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the can's internal lacquer, the valve cup, stem, gasket and dip tube, and the actuator nozzle, every one of which the formulation wets or passes through on its way into the air. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

| analyte | value (µg/kg) | provenance | binding source | component logic |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pb | 500 | read-across | co2022_pb_cannabis_inhaled_nasal_500 (US-CO) |  |
| Cd | 200 | read-across | co2022_cd_cannabis_inhaled_nasal_200 (US-CO) |  |
| tAs | 200 | read-across | co2022_tas_cannabis_inhaled_nasal_200 (US-CO) |  |
| iAs | control | control | bounded by tAs | No government sets an inorganic-arsenic content maximum for any inhale |
| tHg | 100 | read-across | co2022_thg_cannabis_inhaled_nasal_100 (US-CO) |  |
| MeHg | n/m | not-material | bounded by tHg | Methylmercury is formed by microbial methylation in aquatic sediment a |
| Ni | control | control | — | No government sets a nickel content maximum for any inhaled consumer p |
| Sn | control | control | — | No government sets a total-tin content maximum for any inhaled consume |
| Al | control | control | — | No government sets an aluminium content maximum for any inhaled consum |
| Cr | screen 600 | read-across | md2025_cr_total_cannabis_inhalation_600 (US-MD) |  |
| Cr-VI | control | control | — | Hexavalent chromium is never a panel screen number in this programme;  |

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a lead content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 500 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.5 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_pb_inhalable_cannabis_0_5ugg), so two sub-national regulators converge on this value. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 1000 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg (equal but route-unspecific), Nevada 1200 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 5000 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the filled formulation as dispensed, including propellant by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. DISPOSITION OF THE ADDITIONAL ANALYSES THIS CELL MANDATES. This cell requires one analysis beyond the published test, and it carries a criterion and a lot consequence, so that no mandatory analysis on this row is an obligation without an outcome. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published ceiling of 500 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a tinplate or aluminium aerosol can, a nickel-plated or chrome-plated actuator trim, a valve cup or a pigmented moulded housing is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. For lead and this row's components, branch (a) is EMPTY, and the instruments checked before saying so are named rather than waved at: EU RoHS 2011/65/EU is scoped to the homogeneous materials of electrical and electronic equipment and this row carries no powered sub-assembly; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries is scoped to batteries and this row contains none; REACH Annex XVII entry 63 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2015/628 (eu_2015_628_pb_mouthable_general_public_articles_0_05pct) reaches only articles supplied to the general public that may be placed in the mouth by children; Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 reaches consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles and does not reach a packaging, sachet or decorative textile that is not worn; the EU Toy Safety Directive 2009/48/EC with EN 71-3, the CPSIA surface-coating limit and the Canadian and Australian toy-coating limits reach accessible toy material and toy surface coatings, and none of these articles is a toy; 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13) reaches only the metal of a candlewick core and this row has no wick core; the food-contact ceramic, glassware and enamelware instruments reach only articles intended to come into contact with food. Every component result on this row therefore falls to branch (b) or branch (c), and neither leaves a result without a criterion or without a lot consequence. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the can's internal lacquer, the valve cup, stem, gasket and dip tube, and the actuator nozzle, every one of which the formulation wets or passes through on its way into the air), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum number of actuations, whichever is the more demanding, so that any lead transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for lead, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the fragrance house, the solvent and propellant supplier, the can and internal-lacquer maker, the valve maker and the actuator moulder; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a lead content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one.
  - **Cd substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a cadmium content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 400 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 820 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 500 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the can's internal lacquer, the valve cup, stem, gasket and dip tube, and the actuator nozzle, every one of which the formulation wets or passes through on its way into the air), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum number of actuations, whichever is the more demanding, so that any arsenic transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 200 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for total arsenic, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the fragrance house, the solvent and propellant supplier, the can and internal-lacquer maker, the valve maker and the actuator moulder; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a total-arsenic content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one.
  - **tHg substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a total mercury content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 100 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.1 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_thg_inhalable_cannabis_0_1ugg); California's own non-inhalable value for the same products is 3000 ug/kg, thirty times looser. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 200 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 400 ug/kg; Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 sits at 100 ug/kg, equal to the selected value, and California DCC section 15723 also sits at 100 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the filled formulation as dispensed, including propellant by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. ### electric-plug-in-evaporators — Electric plug-in evaporators
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the refill fragrance oil charge drawn up a wick and evaporated from a mains-powered heated surface, presented in a vial seated in a moulded housing with a resistive or PTC heater and a mains plug
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the refill fragrance oil; the wick; the refill vial, cap and gasket; the PTC or resistive heater element; the printed circuit assembly and its solder joints; the mains plug pins, contacts and any nickel or tin plating; and the moulded housing with its pigment masterbatch and any decorative coating. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the wick, the refill vial with its cap and gasket, and the heated surface the wick contacts. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

It is applied in its own basis at its own threshold, on the component it actually governs, and an exceedance blocks certification of the lot outright irrespective of the emitting-material result. That article-and-component value is never republished as this row's emitting-material limit, because it is three to four orders of magnitude looser and republishing it would misrepresent a component prohibition as a product permission. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the wick, the refill vial with its cap and gasket, and the heated surface the wick contacts), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum continuous run of the heater, whichever is the more demanding, so that any cadmium transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 200 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for cadmium, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the fragrance house, the wick supplier, the vial and closure moulder, the heater-element maker, the electronics contract manufacturer and the housing moulder with its pigment-masterbatch supplier; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a cadmium content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one.
  - **tAs substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a total arsenic content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 200 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.2 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_tas_inhalable_cannabis_0_2ugg); California's own non-inhalable value for the same products is 1500 ug/kg, 7.5 times looser, which is again the route doing the work. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 400 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 2000 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 2500 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the refill fragrance oil charge by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. ### automatic-puff-releasers — Automatic puff releasers
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the pressurised metered cartridge formulation as dispensed, including propellant, released on a timer by a battery-powered actuator in a moulded housing
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the pressurised fragrance cartridge and its propellant; the metering valve, stem and gasket; the actuator nozzle; the timer motor or solenoid and its gear train; the printed circuit assembly and solder joints; the primary or rechargeable cells and their contacts and springs; and the moulded housing with pigment masterbatch. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the cartridge's internal lacquer, the metering valve with its stem and gasket, and the actuator nozzle. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

(a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. Further, Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts lead to 100,000 ug/kg of a portable battery by weight (register row eu_batteries_2023_pb_portable_batteries_100000), and it governs any primary or rechargeable cell supplied with the article, tested on the cell as a whole. Each is applied in its own basis at its own threshold, on the component it actually governs, and an exceedance blocks certification of the lot outright irrespective of the emitting-material result. None of these article-and-component values is ever republished as this row's emitting-material limit, because each is three to four orders of magnitude looser and republishing one would misrepresent a component prohibition as a product permission. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the cartridge's internal lacquer, the metering valve with its stem and gasket, and the actuator nozzle), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum number of metered actuations, whichever is the more demanding, so that any lead transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for lead, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the fragrance house, the propellant and cartridge supplier, the metering-valve maker, the electronics contract manufacturer, the cell supplier and the housing moulder; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a lead content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one.
  - **Cd substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a cadmium content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 200 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.2 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_cd_inhalable_cannabis_0_2ugg), so two sub-national regulators converge on this value; California's own non-inhalable value for the same products is 500 ug/kg, 2.5 times looser. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 400 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 820 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 500 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the metered cartridge formulation as dispensed, including propellant by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. ### sensor-puff-releasers — Sensor puff releasers
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the pressurised metered cartridge formulation as dispensed, including propellant, released on passive-infrared or proximity detection by a battery-powered actuator in a moulded housing
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the pressurised fragrance cartridge and its propellant; the metering valve, stem and gasket; the actuator nozzle; the passive-infrared lens and pyroelectric sensor element; the solenoid or motor; the printed circuit assembly and solder joints; the cells, contacts and springs; and the moulded housing with pigment masterbatch. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the cartridge's internal lacquer, the metering valve with its stem and gasket, and the actuator nozzle. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a lead content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 500 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.5 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_pb_inhalable_cannabis_0_5ugg), so two sub-national regulators converge on this value. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 1000 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg (equal but route-unspecific), Nevada 1200 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 5000 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the metered cartridge formulation as dispensed, including propellant by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. DISPOSITION OF THE ADDITIONAL ANALYSES THIS CELL MANDATES. This cell requires one analysis beyond the published test, and it carries a criterion and a lot consequence, so that no mandatory analysis on this row is an obligation without an outcome. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published ceiling of 500 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a metering valve, a pyroelectric sensor element, a solenoid or motor, a solder joint, a cell contact or spring, or a pigmented moulded housing is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. The instruments that reach this row's components for lead are: EU Directive 2011/65/EU (RoHS) restricts lead to 1,000,000 ug/kg of any homogeneous material of electrical and electronic equipment (register row eu_rohs_2011_pb_homogeneous_materials_1000000), and this row's powered sub-assembly is such equipment, so its printed circuit assembly, solder joints, heater or drive element, plated contacts, pins and connectors, and moulded housing are each tested as separate homogeneous materials against that value. Further, Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts lead to 100,000 ug/kg of a portable battery by weight (register row eu_batteries_2023_pb_portable_batteries_100000), and it governs any primary or rechargeable cell supplied with the article, tested on the cell as a whole. Each is applied in its own basis at its own threshold, on the component it actually governs, and an exceedance blocks certification of the lot outright irrespective of the emitting-material result. None of these article-and-component values is ever republished as this row's emitting-material limit, because each is three to four orders of magnitude looser and republishing one would misrepresent a component prohibition as a product permission. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the cartridge's internal lacquer, the metering valve with its stem and gasket, and the actuator nozzle), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum number of metered actuations, whichever is the more demanding, so that any lead transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for lead, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the fragrance house, the propellant and cartridge supplier, the metering-valve maker, the sensor and electronics contract manufacturer, the cell supplier and the housing moulder; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a lead content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one.

For total arsenic and this row's components, branch (a) is EMPTY, and the instruments checked before saying so are named rather than waved at: EU RoHS 2011/65/EU restricts lead, cadmium, mercury and hexavalent chromium and restricts arsenic in no form, so it does not reach this cell even on this row's powered sub-assembly; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts mercury, cadmium and lead only, so it does not reach this cell even on the primary or rechargeable cells this row carries; REACH Annex XVII entry 63 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2015/628 (eu_2015_628_pb_mouthable_general_public_articles_0_05pct) reaches only articles supplied to the general public that may be placed in the mouth by children; Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 reaches consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles and does not reach a packaging, sachet or decorative textile that is not worn; the EU Toy Safety Directive 2009/48/EC with EN 71-3, the CPSIA surface-coating limit and the Canadian and Australian toy-coating limits reach accessible toy material and toy surface coatings, and none of these articles is a toy; 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13) reaches only the metal of a candlewick core and this row has no wick core; the food-contact ceramic, glassware and enamelware instruments reach only articles intended to come into contact with food. Every component result on this row therefore falls to branch (b) or branch (c), and neither leaves a result without a criterion or without a lot consequence. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 600 ug/kg, from Maryland (inhalation route). Maryland is the only regulator on the register that publishes a chromium value scoped to the inhalation route. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: no other jurisdiction on the register publishes a chromium value for any inhaled product: California DCC section 15723, the Colorado route table, the Massachusetts protocol, the Nevada action levels and Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 all omit chromium entirely. Maryland's own oral-route and cutaneous-route chromium values for the same products, 1,100,000 ug/kg, are a different route scope and are deliberately not used. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the metered cartridge formulation as dispensed, including propellant by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. DISPOSITION OF THE ADDITIONAL ANALYSES THIS CELL MANDATES. This cell requires one analysis beyond the published test, and it carries a criterion and a lot consequence, so that no mandatory analysis on this row is an obligation without an outcome. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published total-chromium screen of 600 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a metering valve, a pyroelectric sensor element, a solenoid or motor, a solder joint, a cell contact or spring, or a pigmented moulded housing is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. For total chromium and this row's components, branch (a) is EMPTY, and the instruments checked before saying so are named rather than waved at: EU RoHS 2011/65/EU restricts HEXAVALENT chromium in the homogeneous materials of electrical and electronic equipment (eu_rohs_2011_crvi_homogeneous_materials_1000000) and publishes no total-chromium value, so it reaches this row's hexavalent-chromium cell on the powered sub-assembly this row carries and never this total-chromium cell, because a species and its total-element parent are not interchangeable in either direction; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts mercury, cadmium and lead only and publishes no chromium value, so it does not reach this cell even on the primary or rechargeable cells this row carries; REACH Annex XVII entry 63 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2015/628 (eu_2015_628_pb_mouthable_general_public_articles_0_05pct) reaches only articles supplied to the general public that may be placed in the mouth by children; Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 reaches consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles and does not reach a packaging, sachet or decorative textile that is not worn; the EU Toy Safety Directive 2009/48/EC with EN 71-3, the CPSIA surface-coating limit and the Canadian and Australian toy-coating limits reach accessible toy material and toy surface coatings, and none of these articles is a toy; 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13) reaches only the metal of a candlewick core and this row has no wick core; the food-contact ceramic, glassware and enamelware instruments reach only articles intended to come into contact with food. Every component result on this row therefore falls to branch (b) or branch (c), and neither leaves a result without a criterion or without a lot consequence. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the cartridge's internal lacquer, the metering valve with its stem and gasket, and the actuator nozzle), the criterion is the published total-chromium SCREEN of 600 ug/kg, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum number of metered actuations, whichever is the more demanding, so that any chromium transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. The screen is a tripwire and never a pass/fail in its own right. An emitting-material total-chromium result reaching half the screen (300 ug/kg) orders reflex hexavalent-chromium speciation on that lot under the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell, and that cell's disposition then governs the lot: any hexavalent chromium quantifiable above the method limit of quantitation fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the reflex trigger does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for total chromium, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the fragrance house, the propellant and cartridge supplier, the metering-valve maker, the sensor and electronics contract manufacturer, the cell supplier and the housing moulder; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a total-chromium content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one.
  - **Cr-VI cited register row (disclosure — not this cell's anchor):** eu_rohs_2011_crvi_homogeneous_materials_1000000 — 1000000 µg/kg [EU] Cr-VI, basis "homogeneous materials by weight; source unit percent by weight", scope "electrical and electronic equipment homogeneous materials"

### scented-candles — Scented candles
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the unburnt wax-and-fragrance body (wax base, fragrance load, dye or pigment and any hardener, opacifier or stabiliser), burnt through a wick in a glass, ceramic, tinplate or aluminium vessel
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the wax base (paraffin, soy, coconut, palm, beeswax or a blend); the fragrance load; the dye or pigment; any mineral or polymer hardener, opacifier or UV stabiliser; the wick, its core, sustainer tab and adhesive; the glass, ceramic, tinplate or aluminium vessel and any glaze, enamel, lustre, decal or metallic surface coating; and the lid. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. Beyond the components, the post-burn residue and ash are recovered and analysed as a separate matrix. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the wick with its core, sustainer tab and adhesive, and the inner surface of the vessel with any glaze, enamel, lustre or coating on it that the molten pool wets. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a lead content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 500 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.5 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_pb_inhalable_cannabis_0_5ugg), so two sub-national regulators converge on this value. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 1000 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg (equal but route-unspecific), Nevada 1200 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 5000 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the unburnt wax-and-fragrance body, sampled through its full depth by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. Post-burn residue and ash are analysed in addition to the unburnt article, because combustion removes volatile mass and concentrates non-volatile metal in what is left and in what is emitted. DISPOSITION OF THE ADDITIONAL ANALYSES THIS CELL MANDATES. This cell requires two analyses beyond the published test, and each carries a criterion and a lot consequence, so that no mandatory analysis on this row is an obligation without an outcome. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published ceiling of 500 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a tinplate or aluminium vessel, a metal wick core or sustainer tab, or a ceramic glaze, enamel, lustre or metallic surface coating is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. The instrument that reaches this row's components for lead is: the United States CPSC prohibition at 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), which makes a candle whose wick core exceeds 0.06 percent lead by weight of the core metal a banned hazardous substance (register row us_16cfr1500_17a13_pb_lead_cored_candle_wicks_600ppm, 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; every metal-cored wick is sampled separately, its core metal digested and analysed for lead by ICP-MS, and a core above 0.06 percent lead is a prohibited article irrespective of this row's 500 ug/kg emitting-material ceiling. It is applied in its own basis at its own threshold, on the component it actually governs, and an exceedance blocks certification of the lot outright irrespective of the emitting-material result. That article-and-component value is never republished as this row's emitting-material limit, because it is three to four orders of magnitude looser and republishing it would misrepresent a component prohibition as a product permission. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the wick with its core, sustainer tab and adhesive, and the inner surface of the vessel with any glaze, enamel, lustre or coating on it that the molten pool wets), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum burn cycle, whichever is the more demanding, with the sample drawn through the full depth of the remaining body, so that any lead transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for lead, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the wax refiner, the fragrance house, the dye or pigment supplier, the hardener and additive supplier, the wick maker and the vessel, glaze and decoration supplier; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a lead content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one. POST-BURN RESIDUE AND ASH, THE SECOND ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The post-burn residue and ash analysis is a mass-balance check on the published test, not a second ceiling, because the burn removes most of the article's mass, so a concentration expressed per kilogram of ash carries a denominator one to two orders of magnitude smaller than the published basis and is not comparable to it. It is dispositioned in three steps, so that it too carries an outcome. First, the published ceiling of 500 ug/kg governs certification on the emitting material as sampled above: an emitting-material result at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot, whatever the post-burn residue and ash analysis shows. Second, the residue result is reconciled to the published basis: the measured residue concentration is multiplied by the measured residue yield (residue and ash mass divided by initial article mass) to give the mass of element retained per kilogram of the original article, and the balance against the unburnt-article content is the fraction that left the article in the plume; where that reconciliation cannot be closed within the combined expanded uncertainty of the two determinations, or where the residue concentration implies a retention inconsistent with the declared formulation, the published test is treated as having been run on an unrepresentative sample, the lot is held, re-sampled through the full burn depth and re-tested, and a confirmed unrepresentative published test fails the lot. Third, the result itself is dispositioned under the control-and-escalation rule stated above, with CAPA and raw-material traceback on an unexplained step change and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No residue ceiling, emission-rate limit, per-actuation dose limit or indoor-air concentration limit is published for this row, because no credible sovereign government sets one for any product in this category, and none is manufactured from an occupational exposure limit or an ambient-air guideline.

For total arsenic and this row's components, branch (a) is EMPTY, and the instruments checked before saying so are named rather than waved at: EU RoHS 2011/65/EU restricts lead, cadmium, mercury and hexavalent chromium and restricts arsenic in no form, and this row carries no powered sub-assembly in any event; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts mercury, cadmium and lead only, and this row contains no battery in any event; REACH Annex XVII entry 63 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2015/628 (eu_2015_628_pb_mouthable_general_public_articles_0_05pct) reaches only articles supplied to the general public that may be placed in the mouth by children; Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 reaches consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles and does not reach a packaging, sachet or decorative textile that is not worn; the EU Toy Safety Directive 2009/48/EC with EN 71-3, the CPSIA surface-coating limit and the Canadian and Australian toy-coating limits reach accessible toy material and toy surface coatings, and none of these articles is a toy; the food-contact ceramic, glassware and enamelware instruments reach only articles intended to come into contact with food. Every component result on this row therefore falls to branch (b) or branch (c), and neither leaves a result without a criterion or without a lot consequence. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the wick with its core, sustainer tab and adhesive, and the inner surface of the vessel with any glaze, enamel, lustre or coating on it that the molten pool wets), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum burn cycle, whichever is the more demanding, with the sample drawn through the full depth of the remaining body, so that any arsenic transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 200 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for total arsenic, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the wax refiner, the fragrance house, the dye or pigment supplier, the hardener and additive supplier, the wick maker and the vessel, glaze and decoration supplier; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a total-arsenic content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one. POST-BURN RESIDUE AND ASH, THE SECOND ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The post-burn residue and ash analysis is a mass-balance check on the published test, not a second ceiling, because the burn removes most of the article's mass, so a concentration expressed per kilogram of ash carries a denominator one to two orders of magnitude smaller than the published basis and is not comparable to it. It is dispositioned in three steps, so that it too carries an outcome. First, the published ceiling of 200 ug/kg governs certification on the emitting material as sampled above: an emitting-material result at or above 200 ug/kg fails the lot, whatever the post-burn residue and ash analysis shows. Second, the residue result is reconciled to the published basis: the measured residue concentration is multiplied by the measured residue yield (residue and ash mass divided by initial article mass) to give the mass of element retained per kilogram of the original article, and the balance against the unburnt-article content is the fraction that left the article in the plume; where that reconciliation cannot be closed within the combined expanded uncertainty of the two determinations, or where the residue concentration implies a retention inconsistent with the declared formulation, the published test is treated as having been run on an unrepresentative sample, the lot is held, re-sampled through the full burn depth and re-tested, and a confirmed unrepresentative published test fails the lot. Third, the result itself is dispositioned under the control-and-escalation rule stated above, with CAPA and raw-material traceback on an unexplained step change and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No residue ceiling, emission-rate limit, per-actuation dose limit or indoor-air concentration limit is published for this row, because no credible sovereign government sets one for any product in this category, and none is manufactured from an occupational exposure limit or an ambient-air guideline.
