The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 100 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.1 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_thg_inhalable_cannabis_0_1ugg); California's own non-inhalable value for the same products is 3000 ug/kg, thirty times looser. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 200 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 400 ug/kg; Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 sits at 100 ug/kg, equal to the selected value, and California DCC section 15723 also sits at 100 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the unburnt wax-and-fragrance body, sampled through its full depth by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. Post-burn residue and ash are analysed in addition to the unburnt article, because combustion removes volatile mass and concentrates non-volatile metal in what is left and in what is emitted. ### scented-wax-blocks-melts — Scented wax blocks/wax melts
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the unburnt wax melt body (wax base, fragrance load, dye or pigment and any effect additive or inclusion), melted without a wick in a separately supplied warmer
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the wax base (paraffin, soy, coconut, palm or a blend); the fragrance load; the dye or pigment; any hardener, opacifier, glitter or mica-effect additive; embedded botanical or mineral inclusions; and the clamshell, tray or film packaging in direct contact with the wax. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the clamshell, tray or film in direct contact with the wax, and any embedded botanical or mineral inclusion that melts with it. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

(b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the clamshell, tray or film in direct contact with the wax, and any embedded botanical or mineral inclusion that melts with it), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum melt cycle in the warmer the manufacturer specifies, whichever is the more demanding, so that any lead transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for lead, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the wax refiner, the fragrance house, the dye or pigment and effect-additive supplier, the inclusion supplier and the packaging converter; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a lead content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one.
  - **Cd substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a cadmium content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 200 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.2 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_cd_inhalable_cannabis_0_2ugg), so two sub-national regulators converge on this value; California's own non-inhalable value for the same products is 500 ug/kg, 2.5 times looser. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 400 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 820 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 500 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the unburnt wax melt body, sampled across the full block or across a representative set of cubes by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. ### heated-oil-diffusers — Heated oil diffusers
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the fragrance oil charge, with water or carrier where used, evaporated from a heated ceramic, glass, stone or metal reservoir dish over a tealight or an electric element
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the oil charge; the water or carrier where used; the ceramic, glass, stone or metal reservoir dish and its glaze, frit or coating; the heater element or tealight housing; the printed circuit assembly and solder joints on powered variants; the mains adaptor, USB port or cells; and the moulded or metal housing with its finish. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. Beyond the components, the residue film recovered from the reservoir dish is analysed as a separate matrix. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the reservoir dish with its glaze, frit or coating, and the heated surface the charge stands on. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a lead content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 500 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.5 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_pb_inhalable_cannabis_0_5ugg), so two sub-national regulators converge on this value. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 1000 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg (equal but route-unspecific), Nevada 1200 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 5000 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the oil charge as supplied or as specified for use, plus the residue film recovered from the reservoir dish by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. DISPOSITION OF THE ADDITIONAL ANALYSES THIS CELL MANDATES. This cell requires two analyses beyond the published test, and each carries a criterion and a lot consequence, so that no mandatory analysis on this row is an obligation without an outcome. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published ceiling of 500 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a metal reservoir dish, a ceramic glaze or frit, a heater element, a solder joint, a mains adaptor or USB port, or a metal housing finish is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. The instruments that reach this row's components for lead are: EU Directive 2011/65/EU (RoHS) restricts lead to 1,000,000 ug/kg of any homogeneous material of electrical and electronic equipment (register row eu_rohs_2011_pb_homogeneous_materials_1000000), and this row's powered sub-assembly is such equipment, so its printed circuit assembly, solder joints, heater or drive element, plated contacts, pins and connectors, and moulded housing are each tested as separate homogeneous materials against that value. Further, Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts lead to 100,000 ug/kg of a portable battery by weight (register row eu_batteries_2023_pb_portable_batteries_100000), and it governs any primary or rechargeable cell supplied with the article, tested on the cell as a whole. Each is applied in its own basis at its own threshold, on the component it actually governs, and an exceedance blocks certification of the lot outright irrespective of the emitting-material result. None of these article-and-component values is ever republished as this row's emitting-material limit, because each is three to four orders of magnitude looser and republishing one would misrepresent a component prohibition as a product permission. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the reservoir dish with its glaze, frit or coating, and the heated surface the charge stands on), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum heated run, whichever is the more demanding, so that any lead transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for lead, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the oil and fragrance supplier, the reservoir and glaze maker, the heater-element maker, the electronics contract manufacturer on powered variants and the housing finisher; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a lead content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one. RESERVOIR RESIDUE FILM, THE SECOND ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The reservoir residue-film analysis is a mass-balance check on the published test, not a second ceiling, because the film is emitting material that has concentrated in place after its volatile mass has evaporated, so a concentration expressed per kilogram of film carries a denominator far smaller than the published basis and is not comparable to it. It is dispositioned in three steps, so that it too carries an outcome. First, the published ceiling of 500 ug/kg governs certification on the emitting material as sampled above: an emitting-material result at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot, whatever the reservoir residue-film analysis shows. Second, the result is reconciled to the published basis and the reconciled total is what the published value is applied to: the measured film concentration is multiplied by the recovered film mass and divided by the mass of charge run through the reservoir, to give the element recovered from the film per kilogram of charge; where that figure does not exceed the sealed charge's own content, the film is the charge's own non-volatile fraction concentrated in place and no reservoir contribution is demonstrated, while any excess over the sealed charge's content beyond the combined expanded uncertainty of the two determinations is element the charge has picked up from the reservoir dish, its glaze, frit or coating, and that excess is added to the sealed charge's content to give the total presented to the heated surface and through it to the air; a reconciled total at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot even where the sealed charge on its own was compliant, because the element the charge picks up on its way to the air is element the user inhales; and a pick-up contribution that the manufacturer cannot attribute to a controlled source blocks certification until the contributing component is remediated or substituted. Where the recovered mass cannot be captured and weighed to the precision this comparison requires, the lot is held, re-sampled from a fresh sealed charge and re-tested, and a determination that still cannot be closed fails the lot. Third, the result itself is dispositioned under the control-and-escalation rule stated above, with CAPA and raw-material traceback on an unexplained step change and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No residue ceiling, emission-rate limit, per-actuation dose limit or indoor-air concentration limit is published for this row, because no credible sovereign government sets one for any product in this category, and none is manufactured from an occupational exposure limit or an ambient-air guideline.
  - **Cd substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a cadmium content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. For total arsenic and this row's components, branch (a) is EMPTY, and the instruments checked before saying so are named rather than waved at: EU RoHS 2011/65/EU restricts lead, cadmium, mercury and hexavalent chromium and restricts arsenic in no form, so it does not reach this cell even on this row's powered sub-assembly; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts mercury, cadmium and lead only, so it does not reach this cell even on the primary or rechargeable cells this row carries; REACH Annex XVII entry 63 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2015/628 (eu_2015_628_pb_mouthable_general_public_articles_0_05pct) reaches only articles supplied to the general public that may be placed in the mouth by children; Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 reaches consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles and does not reach a packaging, sachet or decorative textile that is not worn; the EU Toy Safety Directive 2009/48/EC with EN 71-3, the CPSIA surface-coating limit and the Canadian and Australian toy-coating limits reach accessible toy material and toy surface coatings, and none of these articles is a toy; 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13) reaches only the metal of a candlewick core and this row has no wick core; the food-contact ceramic, glassware and enamelware instruments reach only articles intended to come into contact with food. Every component result on this row therefore falls to branch (b) or branch (c), and neither leaves a result without a criterion or without a lot consequence. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 200 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 400 ug/kg; Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 sits at 100 ug/kg, equal to the selected value, and California DCC section 15723 also sits at 100 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the oil charge as supplied or as specified for use, plus the residue film recovered from the reservoir dish by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. DISPOSITION OF THE ADDITIONAL ANALYSES THIS CELL MANDATES. This cell requires two analyses beyond the published test, and each carries a criterion and a lot consequence, so that no mandatory analysis on this row is an obligation without an outcome. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published ceiling of 100 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a metal reservoir dish, a ceramic glaze or frit, a heater element, a solder joint, a mains adaptor or USB port, or a metal housing finish is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. The instruments that reach this row's components for total mercury are: EU Directive 2011/65/EU (RoHS) restricts mercury to 1,000,000 ug/kg of any homogeneous material of electrical and electronic equipment (register row eu_rohs_2011_thg_homogeneous_materials_1000000), and this row's powered sub-assembly is such equipment, so its printed circuit assembly, solder joints, heater or drive element, plated contacts, pins and connectors, and moulded housing are each tested as separate homogeneous materials against that value. Further, Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts mercury to 5,000 ug/kg of a battery by weight (register row eu_batteries_2023_thg_batteries_5000), and it governs any primary or rechargeable cell supplied with the article, tested on the cell as a whole. Each is applied in its own basis at its own threshold, on the component it actually governs, and an exceedance blocks certification of the lot outright irrespective of the emitting-material result. None of these article-and-component values is ever republished as this row's emitting-material limit, because each is three to four orders of magnitude looser and republishing one would misrepresent a component prohibition as a product permission. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the reservoir dish with its glaze, frit or coating, and the heated surface the charge stands on), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum heated run, whichever is the more demanding, so that any mercury transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 100 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for total mercury, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the oil and fragrance supplier, the reservoir and glaze maker, the heater-element maker, the electronics contract manufacturer on powered variants and the housing finisher; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a total-mercury content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one. RESERVOIR RESIDUE FILM, THE SECOND ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The reservoir residue-film analysis is a mass-balance check on the published test, not a second ceiling, because the film is emitting material that has concentrated in place after its volatile mass has evaporated, so a concentration expressed per kilogram of film carries a denominator far smaller than the published basis and is not comparable to it. It is dispositioned in three steps, so that it too carries an outcome. First, the published ceiling of 100 ug/kg governs certification on the emitting material as sampled above: an emitting-material result at or above 100 ug/kg fails the lot, whatever the reservoir residue-film analysis shows. Second, the result is reconciled to the published basis and the reconciled total is what the published value is applied to: the measured film concentration is multiplied by the recovered film mass and divided by the mass of charge run through the reservoir, to give the element recovered from the film per kilogram of charge; where that figure does not exceed the sealed charge's own content, the film is the charge's own non-volatile fraction concentrated in place and no reservoir contribution is demonstrated, while any excess over the sealed charge's content beyond the combined expanded uncertainty of the two determinations is element the charge has picked up from the reservoir dish, its glaze, frit or coating, and that excess is added to the sealed charge's content to give the total presented to the heated surface and through it to the air; a reconciled total at or above 100 ug/kg fails the lot even where the sealed charge on its own was compliant, because the element the charge picks up on its way to the air is element the user inhales; and a pick-up contribution that the manufacturer cannot attribute to a controlled source blocks certification until the contributing component is remediated or substituted. Where the recovered mass cannot be captured and weighed to the precision this comparison requires, the lot is held, re-sampled from a fresh sealed charge and re-tested, and a determination that still cannot be closed fails the lot. Third, the result itself is dispositioned under the control-and-escalation rule stated above, with CAPA and raw-material traceback on an unexplained step change and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No residue ceiling, emission-rate limit, per-actuation dose limit or indoor-air concentration limit is published for this row, because no credible sovereign government sets one for any product in this category, and none is manufactured from an occupational exposure limit or an ambient-air guideline.

### nebula-diffusers — Nebula diffusers
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the oil or oil-and-water charge aerosolised by a piezoelectric transducer through a mesh, orifice or nozzle, or by a venturi pump on nebulising units
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the oil or oil-and-water charge; the piezoelectric transducer disc, its electrodes and any solder or conductive adhesive; the mesh, orifice or nozzle; the water reservoir and any metal fill tube or float sensor; the pump, tubing and glass reservoir on venturi units; the printed circuit assembly, solder joints and LED assembly; the cells or mains adaptor; and the moulded housing. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. Beyond the components, the rinsate recovered from the wetted path (reservoir, mesh and nozzle) is analysed as a separate matrix. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the transducer disc with its electrodes and any solder or conductive adhesive the charge wets, the mesh, orifice or nozzle, the reservoir with any metal fill tube or float sensor, and the pump, tubing and glass reservoir on venturi units. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 200 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.2 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_cd_inhalable_cannabis_0_2ugg), so two sub-national regulators converge on this value; California's own non-inhalable value for the same products is 500 ug/kg, 2.5 times looser. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 400 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 820 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 500 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the oil or oil-and-water charge as specified for use, plus the wetted-path rinsate recovered from reservoir, mesh and nozzle by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. DISPOSITION OF THE ADDITIONAL ANALYSES THIS CELL MANDATES. This cell requires two analyses beyond the published test, and each carries a criterion and a lot consequence, so that no mandatory analysis on this row is an obligation without an outcome. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published ceiling of 200 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a piezoelectric transducer disc with its electrodes, a solder joint or conductive adhesive, a metal mesh or orifice, a metal fill tube or float sensor, or a pump and its tubing is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. The instruments that reach this row's components for cadmium are: EU Directive 2011/65/EU (RoHS) restricts cadmium to 100,000 ug/kg of any homogeneous material of electrical and electronic equipment (register row eu_rohs_2011_cd_homogeneous_materials_100000), and this row's powered sub-assembly is such equipment, so its printed circuit assembly, solder joints, heater or drive element, plated contacts, pins and connectors, and moulded housing are each tested as separate homogeneous materials against that value. Further, Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts cadmium to 20,000 ug/kg of a portable battery by weight (register row eu_batteries_2023_cd_portable_batteries_20000), and it governs any primary or rechargeable cell supplied with the article, tested on the cell as a whole. Each is applied in its own basis at its own threshold, on the component it actually governs, and an exceedance blocks certification of the lot outright irrespective of the emitting-material result. None of these article-and-component values is ever republished as this row's emitting-material limit, because each is three to four orders of magnitude looser and republishing one would misrepresent a component prohibition as a product permission. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the transducer disc with its electrodes and any solder or conductive adhesive the charge wets, the mesh, orifice or nozzle, the reservoir with any metal fill tube or float sensor, and the pump, tubing and glass reservoir on venturi units), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum run, whichever is the more demanding, so that any cadmium transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 200 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for cadmium, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the oil supplier, the transducer and mesh maker, the pump and tubing supplier, the electronics contract manufacturer and the housing moulder; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a cadmium content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one. WETTED-PATH RINSATE, THE SECOND ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The wetted-path rinsate analysis is a mass-balance check on the published test, not a second ceiling, because the rinsate recovers what the charge has picked up from the reservoir, transducer, mesh and nozzle and concentrates it into a small recovered volume, so its concentration is not comparable to the published per-kilogram-of-charge basis. It is dispositioned in three steps, so that it too carries an outcome. First, the published ceiling of 200 ug/kg governs certification on the emitting material as sampled above: an emitting-material result at or above 200 ug/kg fails the lot, whatever the wetted-path rinsate analysis shows. Second, the result is reconciled to the published basis and the reconciled total is what the published value is applied to: the measured rinsate concentration is multiplied by the recovered rinsate mass and divided by the mass of charge run through the wetted path, to give the element recovered from the wetted path per kilogram of charge; where that figure does not exceed the sealed charge's own content, the rinsate is the charge's own residue and no wetted-path contribution is demonstrated, while any excess over the sealed charge's content beyond the combined expanded uncertainty of the two determinations is element the charge has picked up from the transducer, its electrodes, any solder or conductive adhesive, the mesh, orifice or nozzle, the reservoir or any metal fill tube, and that excess is added to the sealed charge's content to give the total presented to the aerosolising element; a reconciled total at or above 200 ug/kg fails the lot even where the sealed charge on its own was compliant, because the element the charge picks up on its way to the air is element the user inhales; and a pick-up contribution that the manufacturer cannot attribute to a controlled source blocks certification until the contributing component is remediated or substituted. Where the recovered mass cannot be captured and weighed to the precision this comparison requires, the lot is held, re-sampled with a fresh charge through a cleaned wetted path and re-tested, and a determination that still cannot be closed fails the lot. Third, the result itself is dispositioned under the control-and-escalation rule stated above, with CAPA and raw-material traceback on an unexplained step change and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No residue ceiling, emission-rate limit, per-actuation dose limit or indoor-air concentration limit is published for this row, because no credible sovereign government sets one for any product in this category, and none is manufactured from an occupational exposure limit or an ambient-air guideline.
  - **tAs substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a total arsenic content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. For total chromium and this row's components, branch (a) is EMPTY, and the instruments checked before saying so are named rather than waved at: EU RoHS 2011/65/EU restricts HEXAVALENT chromium in the homogeneous materials of electrical and electronic equipment (eu_rohs_2011_crvi_homogeneous_materials_1000000) and publishes no total-chromium value, so it reaches this row's hexavalent-chromium cell on the powered sub-assembly this row carries and never this total-chromium cell, because a species and its total-element parent are not interchangeable in either direction; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts mercury, cadmium and lead only and publishes no chromium value, so it does not reach this cell even on the primary or rechargeable cells this row carries; REACH Annex XVII entry 63 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2015/628 (eu_2015_628_pb_mouthable_general_public_articles_0_05pct) reaches only articles supplied to the general public that may be placed in the mouth by children; Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 reaches consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles and does not reach a packaging, sachet or decorative textile that is not worn; the EU Toy Safety Directive 2009/48/EC with EN 71-3, the CPSIA surface-coating limit and the Canadian and Australian toy-coating limits reach accessible toy material and toy surface coatings, and none of these articles is a toy; 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13) reaches only the metal of a candlewick core and this row has no wick core; the food-contact ceramic, glassware and enamelware instruments reach only articles intended to come into contact with food. Every component result on this row therefore falls to branch (b) or branch (c), and neither leaves a result without a criterion or without a lot consequence. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the transducer disc with its electrodes and any solder or conductive adhesive the charge wets, the mesh, orifice or nozzle, the reservoir with any metal fill tube or float sensor, and the pump, tubing and glass reservoir on venturi units), the criterion is the published total-chromium SCREEN of 600 ug/kg, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum run, whichever is the more demanding, so that any chromium transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. The screen is a tripwire and never a pass/fail in its own right. An emitting-material total-chromium result reaching half the screen (300 ug/kg) orders reflex hexavalent-chromium speciation on that lot under the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell, and that cell's disposition then governs the lot: any hexavalent chromium quantifiable above the method limit of quantitation fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the reflex trigger does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for total chromium, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the oil supplier, the transducer and mesh maker, the pump and tubing supplier, the electronics contract manufacturer and the housing moulder; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a total-chromium content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one. WETTED-PATH RINSATE, THE SECOND ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The wetted-path rinsate analysis is a mass-balance check on the published test, not a second ceiling, because the rinsate recovers what the charge has picked up from the reservoir, transducer, mesh and nozzle and concentrates it into a small recovered volume, so its concentration is not comparable to the published per-kilogram-of-charge basis. It is dispositioned in three steps, so that it too carries an outcome. First, the published total-chromium screen of 600 ug/kg governs certification on the emitting material as sampled above: an emitting-material total-chromium result reaching half the screen (300 ug/kg) orders reflex hexavalent-chromium speciation and the Cr-VI cell's zero-tolerance disposition then governs the lot, whatever the wetted-path rinsate analysis shows. Second, the result is reconciled to the published basis and the reconciled total is what the published value is applied to: the measured rinsate concentration is multiplied by the recovered rinsate mass and divided by the mass of charge run through the wetted path, to give the element recovered from the wetted path per kilogram of charge; where that figure does not exceed the sealed charge's own content, the rinsate is the charge's own residue and no wetted-path contribution is demonstrated, while any excess over the sealed charge's content beyond the combined expanded uncertainty of the two determinations is element the charge has picked up from the transducer, its electrodes, any solder or conductive adhesive, the mesh, orifice or nozzle, the reservoir or any metal fill tube, and that excess is added to the sealed charge's content to give the total presented to the aerosolising element; a reconciled total at or above half the screen (300 ug/kg) orders reflex hexavalent-chromium speciation and the Cr-VI cell's disposition then governs, and a reconciled total at or above the screen of 600 ug/kg additionally holds the lot even where the sealed charge on its own was compliant, because the element the charge picks up on its way to the air is element the user inhales; and a pick-up contribution that the manufacturer cannot attribute to a controlled source blocks certification until the contributing component is remediated or substituted. Where the recovered mass cannot be captured and weighed to the precision this comparison requires, the lot is held, re-sampled with a fresh charge through a cleaned wetted path and re-tested, and a determination that still cannot be closed fails the lot. Third, the result itself is dispositioned under the control-and-escalation rule stated above, with CAPA and raw-material traceback on an unexplained step change and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No residue ceiling, emission-rate limit, per-actuation dose limit or indoor-air concentration limit is published for this row, because no credible sovereign government sets one for any product in this category, and none is manufactured from an occupational exposure limit or an ambient-air guideline.
  - **Cr-VI cited register row (disclosure — not this cell's anchor):** eu_rohs_2011_crvi_homogeneous_materials_1000000 — 1000000 µg/kg [EU] Cr-VI, basis "homogeneous materials by weight; source unit percent by weight", scope "electrical and electronic equipment homogeneous materials"

### gel-diffusers — Gel diffusers
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the gel mass as supplied (gelling agent, fragrance load, water and humectant, preservative and chelant system and dye), evaporating passively from a vented cup
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the gelling agent; the fragrance load; water and humectant; the preservative and chelant system; the dye; and the plastic or glass cup, the vented lid, and any membrane, wick pad or fibre insert. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the cup, the vented lid, and any membrane, wick pad or fibre insert the gel wets or the vapour passes through. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a lead content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 500 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.5 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_pb_inhalable_cannabis_0_5ugg), so two sub-national regulators converge on this value. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 1000 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg (equal but route-unspecific), Nevada 1200 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 5000 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the gel mass as supplied, sampled through its full depth by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.1 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_thg_inhalable_cannabis_0_1ugg); California's own non-inhalable value for the same products is 3000 ug/kg, thirty times looser. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 200 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 400 ug/kg; Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 sits at 100 ug/kg, equal to the selected value, and California DCC section 15723 also sits at 100 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the gel mass as supplied, sampled through its full depth by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. DISPOSITION OF THE ADDITIONAL ANALYSES THIS CELL MANDATES. This cell requires one analysis beyond the published test, and it carries a criterion and a lot consequence, so that no mandatory analysis on this row is an obligation without an outcome. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published ceiling of 100 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a metallised or pigmented cup or vented lid, or a metal-bearing membrane or fibre insert is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. For total mercury and this row's components, branch (a) is EMPTY, and the instruments checked before saying so are named rather than waved at: EU RoHS 2011/65/EU is scoped to the homogeneous materials of electrical and electronic equipment and this row carries no powered sub-assembly; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries is scoped to batteries and this row contains none; REACH Annex XVII entry 63 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2015/628 (eu_2015_628_pb_mouthable_general_public_articles_0_05pct) reaches only articles supplied to the general public that may be placed in the mouth by children; Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 reaches consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles and does not reach a packaging, sachet or decorative textile that is not worn; the EU Toy Safety Directive 2009/48/EC with EN 71-3, the CPSIA surface-coating limit and the Canadian and Australian toy-coating limits reach accessible toy material and toy surface coatings, and none of these articles is a toy; 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13) reaches only the metal of a candlewick core and this row has no wick core; the food-contact ceramic, glassware and enamelware instruments reach only articles intended to come into contact with food. Every component result on this row therefore falls to branch (b) or branch (c), and neither leaves a result without a criterion or without a lot consequence. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the cup, the vented lid, and any membrane, wick pad or fibre insert the gel wets or the vapour passes through), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life at the declared storage conditions, or after the declared maximum in-use period, whichever is the more demanding, so that any mercury transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 100 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for total mercury, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the gelling-agent supplier, the fragrance house, the preservative and chelant supplier, the dye supplier and the cup, lid and insert moulder; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a total-mercury content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one.

### capillary-reed-wood-diffusers — Capillary reed/wood diffusers
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the fragrance oil charge with its carrier solvent, drawn by capillary action up rattan reeds, fibre sticks or a wood plug from a glass or ceramic vessel
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the fragrance oil and its carrier solvent (typically dipropylene glycol, isoparaffin or an alcohol); the rattan reeds, fibre sticks or wood plug; the glass or ceramic vessel and its glaze, frit, enamel, lustre, paint or decal; the collar, cap and any metal trim; the closure liner; and any dye. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the rattan reeds, fibre sticks or wood plug, the inner surface of the vessel with any glaze, frit, enamel or lustre the oil wets, the collar and the closure liner. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 200 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.2 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_tas_inhalable_cannabis_0_2ugg); California's own non-inhalable value for the same products is 1500 ug/kg, 7.5 times looser, which is again the route doing the work. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 400 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 2000 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 2500 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). ### incense-sticks-cones — Incense sticks/cones
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the unburnt stick, cone or coil including core, combustible paste, resins and oils, surface dye and any tip or head compound, consumed by smouldering combustion
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the bamboo or wood core stick where present; the combustible paste (joss or jigat powder, wood and bark flour, charcoal, clay or gum binder); the resins, absolutes and essential oils; any animal-derived or marine-derived fixative; the surface dye or colour dip; the tip or head compound on cones and coils; and the packaging in contact with the sticks. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. Beyond the components, the post-burn residue and ash are recovered and analysed as a separate matrix. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the bamboo or wood core stick, the combustible paste with its binder, the resins, absolutes and oils, any animal-derived or marine-derived fixative, the surface dye or colour dip and the tip or head compound, every one of which burns. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.
