POST-BURN RESIDUE AND ASH, THE SECOND ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The post-burn residue and ash analysis is a mass-balance check on the published test, not a second ceiling, because the burn removes most of the article's mass, so a concentration expressed per kilogram of ash carries a denominator one to two orders of magnitude smaller than the published basis and is not comparable to it. It is dispositioned in three steps, so that it too carries an outcome. First, the published ceiling of 500 ug/kg governs certification on the emitting material as sampled above: an emitting-material result at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot, whatever the post-burn residue and ash analysis shows. Second, the residue result is reconciled to the published basis: the measured residue concentration is multiplied by the measured residue yield (residue and ash mass divided by initial article mass) to give the mass of element retained per kilogram of the original article, and the balance against the unburnt-article content is the fraction that left the article in the plume; where that reconciliation cannot be closed within the combined expanded uncertainty of the two determinations, or where the residue concentration implies a retention inconsistent with the declared formulation, the published test is treated as having been run on an unrepresentative sample, the lot is held, re-sampled through the full burn depth and re-tested, and a confirmed unrepresentative published test fails the lot. Third, the result itself is dispositioned under the control-and-escalation rule stated above, with CAPA and raw-material traceback on an unexplained step change and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No residue ceiling, emission-rate limit, per-actuation dose limit or indoor-air concentration limit is published for this row, because no credible sovereign government sets one for any product in this category, and none is manufactured from an occupational exposure limit or an ambient-air guideline.
  - **Cd substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a cadmium content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 200 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.2 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_cd_inhalable_cannabis_0_2ugg), so two sub-national regulators converge on this value; California's own non-inhalable value for the same products is 500 ug/kg, 2.5 times looser. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 400 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 820 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 500 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the unburnt stick, cone or coil including core, paste and surface dye, homogenised across the retail unit by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. Post-burn residue and ash are analysed in addition to the unburnt article, because combustion removes volatile mass and concentrates non-volatile metal in what is left and in what is emitted. DISPOSITION OF THE ADDITIONAL ANALYSES THIS CELL MANDATES. This cell requires two analyses beyond the published test, and each carries a criterion and a lot consequence, so that no mandatory analysis on this row is an obligation without an outcome. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published ceiling of 200 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a metallised or pigmented surface dye, a mineral or clay binder fraction, or a metallised packaging film in contact with the sticks is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. For cadmium and this row's components, branch (a) is EMPTY, and the instruments checked before saying so are named rather than waved at: EU RoHS 2011/65/EU is scoped to the homogeneous materials of electrical and electronic equipment and this row carries no powered sub-assembly; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries is scoped to batteries and this row contains none; REACH Annex XVII entry 63 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2015/628 (eu_2015_628_pb_mouthable_general_public_articles_0_05pct) reaches only articles supplied to the general public that may be placed in the mouth by children; Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 reaches consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles and does not reach a packaging, sachet or decorative textile that is not worn; the EU Toy Safety Directive 2009/48/EC with EN 71-3, the CPSIA surface-coating limit and the Canadian and Australian toy-coating limits reach accessible toy material and toy surface coatings, and none of these articles is a toy; 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13) reaches only the metal of a candlewick core and this row has no wick core; the food-contact ceramic, glassware and enamelware instruments reach only articles intended to come into contact with food. Every component result on this row therefore falls to branch (b) or branch (c), and neither leaves a result without a criterion or without a lot consequence. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the bamboo or wood core stick, the combustible paste with its binder, the resins, absolutes and oils, any animal-derived or marine-derived fixative, the surface dye or colour dip and the tip or head compound, every one of which burns), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life, with the sample homogenised across the retail unit rather than drawn from a single stick, so that any cadmium transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 200 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for cadmium, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the bamboo or wood core supplier, the paste, binder and charcoal supplier by country and region of origin, the resin, absolute and essential-oil supplier, the fixative supplier, the dye supplier and the packaging converter; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a cadmium content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: no other jurisdiction on the register publishes a chromium value for any inhaled product: California DCC section 15723, the Colorado route table, the Massachusetts protocol, the Nevada action levels and Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 all omit chromium entirely. Maryland's own oral-route and cutaneous-route chromium values for the same products, 1,100,000 ug/kg, are a different route scope and are deliberately not used. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the unburnt stick, cone or coil including core, paste and surface dye, homogenised across the retail unit by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. Post-burn residue and ash are analysed in addition to the unburnt article, because combustion removes volatile mass and concentrates non-volatile metal in what is left and in what is emitted. DISPOSITION OF THE ADDITIONAL ANALYSES THIS CELL MANDATES. This cell requires two analyses beyond the published test, and each carries a criterion and a lot consequence, so that no mandatory analysis on this row is an obligation without an outcome. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published total-chromium screen of 600 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a metallised or pigmented surface dye, a mineral or clay binder fraction, or a metallised packaging film in contact with the sticks is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. For total chromium and this row's components, branch (a) is EMPTY, and the instruments checked before saying so are named rather than waved at: EU RoHS 2011/65/EU restricts HEXAVALENT chromium in the homogeneous materials of electrical and electronic equipment (eu_rohs_2011_crvi_homogeneous_materials_1000000) and publishes no total-chromium value, so it reaches this row's hexavalent-chromium cell if a powered sub-assembly is ever present and never this total-chromium cell, because a species and its total-element parent are not interchangeable in either direction; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts mercury, cadmium and lead only and publishes no chromium value, and this row contains no battery in any event; REACH Annex XVII entry 63 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2015/628 (eu_2015_628_pb_mouthable_general_public_articles_0_05pct) reaches only articles supplied to the general public that may be placed in the mouth by children; Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 reaches consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles and does not reach a packaging, sachet or decorative textile that is not worn; the EU Toy Safety Directive 2009/48/EC with EN 71-3, the CPSIA surface-coating limit and the Canadian and Australian toy-coating limits reach accessible toy material and toy surface coatings, and none of these articles is a toy; 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13) reaches only the metal of a candlewick core and this row has no wick core; the food-contact ceramic, glassware and enamelware instruments reach only articles intended to come into contact with food. Every component result on this row therefore falls to branch (b) or branch (c), and neither leaves a result without a criterion or without a lot consequence. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the bamboo or wood core stick, the combustible paste with its binder, the resins, absolutes and oils, any animal-derived or marine-derived fixative, the surface dye or colour dip and the tip or head compound, every one of which burns), the criterion is the published total-chromium SCREEN of 600 ug/kg, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life, with the sample homogenised across the retail unit rather than drawn from a single stick, so that any chromium transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. The screen is a tripwire and never a pass/fail in its own right. An emitting-material total-chromium result reaching half the screen (300 ug/kg) orders reflex hexavalent-chromium speciation on that lot under the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell, and that cell's disposition then governs the lot: any hexavalent chromium quantifiable above the method limit of quantitation fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the reflex trigger does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for total chromium, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the bamboo or wood core supplier, the paste, binder and charcoal supplier by country and region of origin, the resin, absolute and essential-oil supplier, the fixative supplier, the dye supplier and the packaging converter; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a total-chromium content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one. POST-BURN RESIDUE AND ASH, THE SECOND ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The post-burn residue and ash analysis is a mass-balance check on the published test, not a second ceiling, because the burn removes most of the article's mass, so a concentration expressed per kilogram of ash carries a denominator one to two orders of magnitude smaller than the published basis and is not comparable to it. It is dispositioned in three steps, so that it too carries an outcome. First, the published total-chromium screen of 600 ug/kg governs certification on the emitting material as sampled above: an emitting-material total-chromium result reaching half the screen (300 ug/kg) orders reflex hexavalent-chromium speciation and the Cr-VI cell's zero-tolerance disposition then governs the lot, whatever the post-burn residue and ash analysis shows. Second, the residue result is reconciled to the published basis: the measured residue concentration is multiplied by the measured residue yield (residue and ash mass divided by initial article mass) to give the mass of element retained per kilogram of the original article, and the balance against the unburnt-article content is the fraction that left the article in the plume; where that reconciliation cannot be closed within the combined expanded uncertainty of the two determinations, or where the residue concentration implies a retention inconsistent with the declared formulation, the published test is treated as having been run on an unrepresentative sample, the lot is held, re-sampled through the full burn depth and re-tested, and a confirmed unrepresentative published test fails the lot. Third, the result itself is dispositioned under the control-and-escalation rule stated above, with CAPA and raw-material traceback on an unexplained step change and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No residue ceiling, emission-rate limit, per-actuation dose limit or indoor-air concentration limit is published for this row, because no credible sovereign government sets one for any product in this category, and none is manufactured from an occupational exposure limit or an ambient-air guideline.

### potpourri-sachets — Potpourri/sachets
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the dried botanical mass with its applied fragrance oil, fixative and dye, evaporating passively through a fabric, paper or mesh sachet
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the dried botanical mix (petals, leaves, bark, cones, pods, husks, wood shavings, citrus peel); any decorative mineral, shell, coral or other marine element; the fragrance oil and fixative; the dye or colour wash; and the fabric, paper or mesh sachet, its thread and any metal or plastic closure or hanging hardware. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the dried botanical mass with its applied oil and dye, any decorative mineral, shell, coral or other marine element in contact with it, and the fabric, paper or mesh through which the fragrance passes. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a lead content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 500 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.5 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_pb_inhalable_cannabis_0_5ugg), so two sub-national regulators converge on this value. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 1000 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg (equal but route-unspecific), Nevada 1200 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 5000 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for total arsenic, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the botanical grower and consolidator by country and region of origin, the fragrance house, the dye supplier, the sachet-fabric supplier and the closure and hardware supplier; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a total-arsenic content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one.
  - **tHg substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a total mercury content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 100 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.1 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_thg_inhalable_cannabis_0_1ugg); California's own non-inhalable value for the same products is 3000 ug/kg, thirty times looser. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 200 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 400 ug/kg; Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 sits at 100 ug/kg, equal to the selected value, and California DCC section 15723 also sits at 100 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the dried botanical mass with its applied oil and dye, homogenised across the retail unit, with the sachet material analysed as a separate component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. ### vacuum-cleaner-fragrance-additives — Vacuum cleaner fragrance additives
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the additive as supplied (carrier granule, bead, powder or pad substrate with its fragrance load, dye and flow agents), drawn into and dispersed by an appliance airstream
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the carrier granule, bead, powder or pad substrate (mineral, zeolite, cellulose, starch or polymer); the fragrance load; the dye; any anti-caking, free-flow or fixative agent; and the sachet, disc backing or dispenser body. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. Beyond the components, the friable fines fraction is separated and analysed in its own right, the published value applying to it directly. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the carrier granule, bead, powder or pad substrate, and the sachet, disc backing or dispenser body through which the additive is drawn into the appliance airstream. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

The friable fines fraction is not a separate matrix requiring a separate criterion: it is this row's emitting material in its most respirable form, and the published ceiling of 500 ug/kg applies to it directly and on its own terms. A fines-fraction result at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot independently of the bulk result, and the bulk result never averages the fines away. Where the fines fraction and the bulk result diverge by more than the combined expanded uncertainty of the two determinations, the lot is held, re-sampled after reconditioning to the declared particle-size distribution and re-tested, and a confirmed enrichment of lead into the respirable fraction that the manufacturer cannot attribute to a controlled source blocks certification. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published ceiling of 200 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a mineral or zeolite carrier substrate, a metal-bearing dye or anti-caking agent, or a pigmented dispenser body is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. For cadmium and this row's components, branch (a) is EMPTY, and the instruments checked before saying so are named rather than waved at: EU RoHS 2011/65/EU is scoped to the homogeneous materials of electrical and electronic equipment and this row carries no powered sub-assembly; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries is scoped to batteries and this row contains none; REACH Annex XVII entry 63 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2015/628 (eu_2015_628_pb_mouthable_general_public_articles_0_05pct) reaches only articles supplied to the general public that may be placed in the mouth by children; Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 reaches consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles and does not reach a packaging, sachet or decorative textile that is not worn; the EU Toy Safety Directive 2009/48/EC with EN 71-3, the CPSIA surface-coating limit and the Canadian and Australian toy-coating limits reach accessible toy material and toy surface coatings, and none of these articles is a toy; 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13) reaches only the metal of a candlewick core and this row has no wick core; the food-contact ceramic, glassware and enamelware instruments reach only articles intended to come into contact with food. Every component result on this row therefore falls to branch (b) or branch (c), and neither leaves a result without a criterion or without a lot consequence. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the carrier granule, bead, powder or pad substrate, and the sachet, disc backing or dispenser body through which the additive is drawn into the appliance airstream), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life at the declared storage conditions, with the sample homogenised across the retail unit, so that any cadmium transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 200 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for cadmium, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the carrier-substrate supplier, the fragrance house, the dye supplier, the anti-caking and fixative supplier and the sachet, backing or dispenser converter; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a cadmium content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one. FRIABLE FINES FRACTION, THE SECOND ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The friable fines fraction is not a separate matrix requiring a separate criterion: it is this row's emitting material in its most respirable form, and the published ceiling of 200 ug/kg applies to it directly and on its own terms. A fines-fraction result at or above 200 ug/kg fails the lot independently of the bulk result, and the bulk result never averages the fines away. Where the fines fraction and the bulk result diverge by more than the combined expanded uncertainty of the two determinations, the lot is held, re-sampled after reconditioning to the declared particle-size distribution and re-tested, and a confirmed enrichment of cadmium into the respirable fraction that the manufacturer cannot attribute to a controlled source blocks certification. The fines result is otherwise dispositioned under the control-and-escalation rule stated above. No emission-rate limit, per-actuation dose limit or indoor-air concentration limit is published for this row, because no credible sovereign government sets one for any product in this category and none is manufactured from an occupational or ambient-air value.
  - **tAs substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a total arsenic content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 200 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.2 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_tas_inhalable_cannabis_0_2ugg); California's own non-inhalable value for the same products is 1500 ug/kg, 7.5 times looser, which is again the route doing the work. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 400 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 2000 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 2500 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the additive as supplied, homogenised across the retail unit, with the friable fines fraction analysed separately by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. ### air-product-refill-materials — Replacement/refill materials for any dispensing system (oil refills, gel refills, spray refills)
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the refill charge as supplied in the physical state in which it is sold (fragrance concentrate with its carrier solvent, gel matrix or propellant), intended to recharge a host air-emission product
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers the fragrance concentrate; the carrier solvent or gel matrix; the propellant where present; the dye; the wick, reed or pad where supplied; and the vial, cartridge, cup, closure, gasket, liner and any metal crimp, seal or foil. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are the wick, reed or pad where supplied, the vial, cartridge or cup, and the closure, gasket, liner and any metal crimp, seal or foil the charge contacts. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a lead content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 500 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.5 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_pb_inhalable_cannabis_0_5ugg), so two sub-national regulators converge on this value. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 1000 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg (equal but route-unspecific), Nevada 1200 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 5000 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the refill charge as supplied, in the physical state in which it is sold by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. DISPOSITION OF THE ADDITIONAL ANALYSES THIS CELL MANDATES. This cell requires one analysis beyond the published test, and it carries a criterion and a lot consequence, so that no mandatory analysis on this row is an obligation without an outcome. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published ceiling of 500 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a metal crimp, seal or foil, a metallised cartridge or cup, or a pigmented closure is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. For lead and this row's components, branch (a) is EMPTY, and the instruments checked before saying so are named rather than waved at: EU RoHS 2011/65/EU is scoped to the homogeneous materials of electrical and electronic equipment and this row carries no powered sub-assembly; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries is scoped to batteries and this row contains none; REACH Annex XVII entry 63 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2015/628 (eu_2015_628_pb_mouthable_general_public_articles_0_05pct) reaches only articles supplied to the general public that may be placed in the mouth by children; Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 reaches consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles and does not reach a packaging, sachet or decorative textile that is not worn; the EU Toy Safety Directive 2009/48/EC with EN 71-3, the CPSIA surface-coating limit and the Canadian and Australian toy-coating limits reach accessible toy material and toy surface coatings, and none of these articles is a toy; 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13) reaches only the metal of a candlewick core and this row has no wick core; the food-contact ceramic, glassware and enamelware instruments reach only articles intended to come into contact with food. Every component result on this row therefore falls to branch (b) or branch (c), and neither leaves a result without a criterion or without a lot consequence. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the wick, reed or pad where supplied, the vial, cartridge or cup, and the closure, gasket, liner and any metal crimp, seal or foil the charge contacts), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life at the declared storage conditions, in the physical state in which the refill is sold, so that any lead transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for lead, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the fragrance house, the carrier-solvent or gel-matrix supplier, the propellant supplier, the wick, reed or pad supplier and the vial, cartridge and closure converter; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a lead content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one.

Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the refill charge as supplied, in the physical state in which it is sold by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. DISPOSITION OF THE ADDITIONAL ANALYSES THIS CELL MANDATES. This cell requires one analysis beyond the published test, and it carries a criterion and a lot consequence, so that no mandatory analysis on this row is an obligation without an outcome. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published total-chromium screen of 600 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a metal crimp, seal or foil, a metallised cartridge or cup, or a pigmented closure is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. For total chromium and this row's components, branch (a) is EMPTY, and the instruments checked before saying so are named rather than waved at: EU RoHS 2011/65/EU restricts HEXAVALENT chromium in the homogeneous materials of electrical and electronic equipment (eu_rohs_2011_crvi_homogeneous_materials_1000000) and publishes no total-chromium value, so it reaches this row's hexavalent-chromium cell if a powered sub-assembly is ever present and never this total-chromium cell, because a species and its total-element parent are not interchangeable in either direction; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts mercury, cadmium and lead only and publishes no chromium value, and this row contains no battery in any event; REACH Annex XVII entry 63 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2015/628 (eu_2015_628_pb_mouthable_general_public_articles_0_05pct) reaches only articles supplied to the general public that may be placed in the mouth by children; Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 reaches consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles and does not reach a packaging, sachet or decorative textile that is not worn; the EU Toy Safety Directive 2009/48/EC with EN 71-3, the CPSIA surface-coating limit and the Canadian and Australian toy-coating limits reach accessible toy material and toy surface coatings, and none of these articles is a toy; 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13) reaches only the metal of a candlewick core and this row has no wick core; the food-contact ceramic, glassware and enamelware instruments reach only articles intended to come into contact with food. Every component result on this row therefore falls to branch (b) or branch (c), and neither leaves a result without a criterion or without a lot consequence. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (the wick, reed or pad where supplied, the vial, cartridge or cup, and the closure, gasket, liner and any metal crimp, seal or foil the charge contacts), the criterion is the published total-chromium SCREEN of 600 ug/kg, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life at the declared storage conditions, in the physical state in which the refill is sold, so that any chromium transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. The screen is a tripwire and never a pass/fail in its own right. An emitting-material total-chromium result reaching half the screen (300 ug/kg) orders reflex hexavalent-chromium speciation on that lot under the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell, and that cell's disposition then governs the lot: any hexavalent chromium quantifiable above the method limit of quantitation fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the reflex trigger does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for total chromium, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to the fragrance house, the carrier-solvent or gel-matrix supplier, the propellant supplier, the wick, reed or pad supplier and the vial, cartridge and closure converter; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a total-chromium content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one.

### home-air-emission-products-other — Other home-air emission products (case-by-case, inhalation prioritized)
- native basis: as sold, micrograms of element per kilogram of the emitting material (the fragrance concentrate, wax, gel, botanical mass or refill charge that is aerosolised, evaporated or combusted), with component-level screening of the article's non-emitting parts; a content screen on the article and its refill, never an emission rate, a per-actuation dose or an indoor-air concentration
- material: the emitting formulation or impregnated substrate identified at Step 0, together with every material in the emission path from reservoir to outlet, recorded on the certificate
- component-level sampling plan: The component-level sampling plan for this row covers determined case-by-case at Step 0 and recorded on the certificate; at minimum the emitting formulation or impregnated substrate, every material in the emission path from reservoir to outlet, the housing and its finish, and any powered, heated or combustion sub-assembly. Every listed component is sampled and tested as a distinct component by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS, and hexavalent-chromium reflex speciation on a component follows the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell. Beyond the components, any post-use residue in the emission path identified at Step 0 is recovered and analysed as a separate matrix. EMISSION PATH. The components in the emission path, which branch (b) of every numeric cell's disposition governs, are every material in the emission path from reservoir to outlet identified at Step 0 and recorded on the certificate. Every component result carries a criterion and a lot consequence under the three ordered branches published on each numeric cell: a sovereign instrument that reaches the component and the analyte governs it in its own basis and an exceedance blocks certification; an emission-path component is governed through the published value applied to emitting material sampled from the finished assembled article; and any remaining component result is held to ALARA against demonstrated process capability, with CAPA, raw-material traceback and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No component ceiling is invented.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a lead content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 500 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.5 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_pb_inhalable_cannabis_0_5ugg), so two sub-national regulators converge on this value. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 1000 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg (equal but route-unspecific), Nevada 1200 ug/kg, Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 5000 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the emitting formulation or impregnated substrate identified at Step 0, plus every material in the emission path by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. DISPOSITION OF THE ADDITIONAL ANALYSES THIS CELL MANDATES. This cell requires two analyses beyond the published test, and each carries a criterion and a lot consequence, so that no mandatory analysis on this row is an obligation without an outcome. COMPONENT RESULTS, THE FIRST ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The published ceiling of 500 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a powered, heated or combustion sub-assembly, a metal or metallised housing finish, or a plated, glazed or coated surface in the emission path, each as identified at Step 0 is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. (a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. The instruments that reach this row's components for lead are: EU Directive 2011/65/EU (RoHS) restricts lead to 1,000,000 ug/kg of any homogeneous material of electrical and electronic equipment (register row eu_rohs_2011_pb_homogeneous_materials_1000000), and this row's powered sub-assembly is such equipment, so its printed circuit assembly, solder joints, heater or drive element, plated contacts, pins and connectors, and moulded housing are each tested as separate homogeneous materials against that value. Further, Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts lead to 100,000 ug/kg of a portable battery by weight (register row eu_batteries_2023_pb_portable_batteries_100000), and it governs any primary or rechargeable cell supplied with the article, tested on the cell as a whole. Further, the United States CPSC prohibition at 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), which makes a candle whose wick core exceeds 0.06 percent lead by weight of the core metal a banned hazardous substance (register row us_16cfr1500_17a13_pb_lead_cored_candle_wicks_600ppm, 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; every metal-cored wick is sampled separately, its core metal digested and analysed for lead by ICP-MS, and a core above 0.06 percent lead is a prohibited article irrespective of this row's 500 ug/kg emitting-material ceiling. Each is applied in its own basis at its own threshold, on the component it actually governs, and an exceedance blocks certification of the lot outright irrespective of the emitting-material result. None of these article-and-component values is ever republished as this row's emitting-material limit, because each is three to four orders of magnitude looser and republishing one would misrepresent a component prohibition as a product permission. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (every material in the emission path from reservoir to outlet identified at Step 0 and recorded on the certificate), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum run, burn or heat condition identified at Step 0, whichever is the more demanding, so that any lead transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for lead, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to every raw-material and component supplier identified at Step 0 and recorded on the certificate, by country and region of origin where the input is agricultural or mineral; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a lead content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one. POST-USE RESIDUE IN THE EMISSION PATH, THE SECOND ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The post-use emission-path residue analysis identified at Step 0 is a mass-balance check on the published test, not a second ceiling, because a residue recovered after use has lost the volatile mass that made up most of the emitting material, so its concentration carries a denominator far smaller than the published basis and is not comparable to it. It is dispositioned in three steps, so that it too carries an outcome. First, the published ceiling of 500 ug/kg governs certification on the emitting material as sampled above: an emitting-material result at or above 500 ug/kg fails the lot, whatever the post-use emission-path residue analysis identified at Step 0 shows. Second, the residue result is reconciled to the published basis: the measured residue concentration is multiplied by the recovered residue mass and divided by the mass of emitting material consumed, to give the mass of element retained per kilogram of the emitting material as supplied, and the balance against the as-supplied content is the fraction that left the article; where that reconciliation cannot be closed within the combined expanded uncertainty of the two determinations, or where the residue concentration implies a retention inconsistent with the declared formulation, the published test is treated as having been run on an unrepresentative sample, the lot is held, re-sampled from a fresh unit under the Step 0 conditions of use and re-tested, and a confirmed unrepresentative published test fails the lot. Third, the result itself is dispositioned under the control-and-escalation rule stated above, with CAPA and raw-material traceback on an unexplained step change and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No residue ceiling, emission-rate limit, per-actuation dose limit or indoor-air concentration limit is published for this row, because no credible sovereign government sets one for any product in this category, and none is manufactured from an occupational exposure limit or an ambient-air guideline.

(b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (every material in the emission path from reservoir to outlet identified at Step 0 and recorded on the certificate), the criterion is the published ceiling itself, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum run, burn or heat condition identified at Step 0, whichever is the more demanding, so that any arsenic transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. An emitting-material result at or above 200 ug/kg fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the ceiling does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for total arsenic, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to every raw-material and component supplier identified at Step 0 and recorded on the certificate, by country and region of origin where the input is agricultural or mineral; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a total-arsenic content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one. POST-USE RESIDUE IN THE EMISSION PATH, THE SECOND ADDITIONAL ANALYSIS THIS CELL MANDATES. The post-use emission-path residue analysis identified at Step 0 is a mass-balance check on the published test, not a second ceiling, because a residue recovered after use has lost the volatile mass that made up most of the emitting material, so its concentration carries a denominator far smaller than the published basis and is not comparable to it. It is dispositioned in three steps, so that it too carries an outcome. First, the published ceiling of 200 ug/kg governs certification on the emitting material as sampled above: an emitting-material result at or above 200 ug/kg fails the lot, whatever the post-use emission-path residue analysis identified at Step 0 shows. Second, the residue result is reconciled to the published basis: the measured residue concentration is multiplied by the recovered residue mass and divided by the mass of emitting material consumed, to give the mass of element retained per kilogram of the emitting material as supplied, and the balance against the as-supplied content is the fraction that left the article; where that reconciliation cannot be closed within the combined expanded uncertainty of the two determinations, or where the residue concentration implies a retention inconsistent with the declared formulation, the published test is treated as having been run on an unrepresentative sample, the lot is held, re-sampled from a fresh unit under the Step 0 conditions of use and re-tested, and a confirmed unrepresentative published test fails the lot. Third, the result itself is dispositioned under the control-and-escalation rule stated above, with CAPA and raw-material traceback on an unexplained step change and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed. No residue ceiling, emission-rate limit, per-actuation dose limit or indoor-air concentration limit is published for this row, because no credible sovereign government sets one for any product in this category, and none is manufactured from an occupational exposure limit or an ambient-air guideline.
  - **tHg substantiation:** No government anywhere sets a total mercury content maximum for an air freshener, plug-in evaporator, puff releaser, candle body, wax melt, oil or nebula diffuser, gel or reed diffuser, incense stick, potpourri, vacuum-cleaner fragrance additive or fragrance refill. The only sovereign heavy-metal instrument in any jurisdiction that names a product in this category is the United States CPSC prohibition on lead-cored candlewicks, 16 CFR 1500.17(a)(13), mirrored by Australia's Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956; it restricts lead in the metal of a wick core and covers no other analyte and no other product form. The cell is therefore a DISCLOSED READ-ACROSS, and it is taken on the axis the regulators themselves used: route of administration. Colorado's 2022 heavy-metal table and Maryland's Technical Authority Rev. 6.0 both publish a route LADDER over the same underlying matrices, with the inhalation row strictest, the oral row looser and the topical or cutaneous row looser still. The value is therefore a function of the route, not of the source matrix's chemistry, and Colorado scopes its strictest row in the instrument's own words to "inhaled product or audited product administered by metered-dose nasal spray", which describes a delivery mechanism this category shares. Maryland's own route gap is the sharpest available evidence for that reading: its inhalation-route total-chromium value is 600 ug/kg while its oral and cutaneous values for the same products are 1,100,000 ug/kg. Selected value 100 ug/kg, from Colorado (co2022 inhaled/nasal row). California DCC section 15723 independently sets the identical 0.1 ug/g for inhalable products (us_ca_dcc_2026_thg_inhalable_cannabis_0_1ugg); California's own non-inhalable value for the same products is 3000 ug/kg, thirty times looser. The looser or route-unspecific values on the register are recorded and NOT selected: Maryland inhalation route 200 ug/kg, Massachusetts all-uses protocol 500 ug/kg, Nevada 400 ug/kg; Israel IMC-GMP Procedure 152 sits at 100 ug/kg, equal to the selected value, and California DCC section 15723 also sits at 100 ug/kg. The basis is identical to this row's native basis, micrograms of element per kilogram of product as tested, so no basis conversion, no reconstitution and no dry-to-wet adjustment is performed. The read-across is published as `read-across` and never as `regulatory-alignment`, because no instrument regulates this exact cell. Adopting it tightens rather than relaxes the applicable regulatory position: it is three to four orders of magnitude stricter than every article-content instrument that actually binds these products (RoHS lead at 1,000,000 ug/kg of homogeneous material, the candlewick-core prohibition threshold at 600,000 ug/kg of wick-core metal, REACH cadmium in polymers at 100,000 ug/kg). No occurrence, percentile, feasibility or precautionary input touched the number in either direction. Determined on the emitting formulation or impregnated substrate identified at Step 0, plus every material in the emission path by microwave-assisted acid digestion and ICP-MS with collision/reaction-cell control of the interfered masses, with the article's non-emitting components digested and screened separately per this row's component plan. The published ceiling of 100 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a powered, heated or combustion sub-assembly, a metal or metallised housing finish, or a plated, glazed or coated surface in the emission path, each as identified at Step 0 is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air. Every component result is instead dispositioned in three ordered branches, and no component result is left outside them. The published total-chromium screen of 600 ug/kg governs the emitting material and is NOT transferred to a component's bulk content: a powered, heated or combustion sub-assembly, a metal or metallised housing finish, or a plated, glazed or coated surface in the emission path, each as identified at Step 0 is or may be substantially metal, metal-plated, metal-glazed or metal-pigmented by construction, and reading an emitting-material value against a structural component's bulk content would be exactly the basis conflation this row's basis guard exists to prevent, failing every compliant article for a metal that never enters the air.
