(a) WHERE A SOVEREIGN INSTRUMENT REACHES THE COMPONENT AND THIS ANALYTE, that instrument is the criterion. For total chromium and this row's components, branch (a) is EMPTY, and the instruments checked before saying so are named rather than waved at: EU RoHS 2011/65/EU restricts HEXAVALENT chromium in the homogeneous materials of electrical and electronic equipment (eu_rohs_2011_crvi_homogeneous_materials_1000000) and publishes no total-chromium value, so it reaches this row's hexavalent-chromium cell on the powered sub-assembly this row carries and never this total-chromium cell, because a species and its total-element parent are not interchangeable in either direction; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542 on batteries restricts mercury, cadmium and lead only and publishes no chromium value, so it does not reach this cell even on the primary or rechargeable cells this row carries; REACH Annex XVII entry 63 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2015/628 (eu_2015_628_pb_mouthable_general_public_articles_0_05pct) reaches only articles supplied to the general public that may be placed in the mouth by children; Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 reaches consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles and does not reach a packaging, sachet or decorative textile that is not worn; the EU Toy Safety Directive 2009/48/EC with EN 71-3, the CPSIA surface-coating limit and the Canadian and Australian toy-coating limits reach accessible toy material and toy surface coatings, and none of these articles is a toy; the food-contact ceramic, glassware and enamelware instruments reach only articles intended to come into contact with food. Every component result on this row therefore falls to branch (b) or branch (c), and neither leaves a result without a criterion or without a lot consequence. (b) WHERE THE COMPONENT IS IN THE EMISSION PATH (every material in the emission path from reservoir to outlet identified at Step 0 and recorded on the certificate), the criterion is the published total-chromium SCREEN of 600 ug/kg, applied where its basis makes it meaningful: the test is performed on emitting material sampled from the finished, filled and assembled article rather than from a pre-fill bulk batch, at the end of the declared shelf life or after the declared maximum run, burn or heat condition identified at Step 0, whichever is the more demanding, so that any chromium transferred out of an emission-path component into the emitting material is carried into the published result rather than escaping it. The screen is a tripwire and never a pass/fail in its own right. An emitting-material total-chromium result reaching half the screen (300 ug/kg) orders reflex hexavalent-chromium speciation on that lot under the reference method published on this row's Cr-VI cell, and that cell's disposition then governs the lot: any hexavalent chromium quantifiable above the method limit of quantitation fails the lot. A rising component result while the emitting material stays below the reflex trigger does not by itself fail the lot and falls to branch (c). (c) OTHERWISE, for a component outside the emission path that no sovereign instrument reaches for total chromium, the component result is dispositioned under the HMTc control-and-escalation rule, in the same terms this row's control cells already use: the result is held to ALARA against the manufacturer's demonstrated process capability for that formulation and component set; a result outside that capability band, or an unexplained step change between lots, triggers CAPA and raw-material traceback to every raw-material and component supplier identified at Step 0 and recorded on the certificate, by country and region of origin where the input is agricultural or mineral; a confirmed uncontrolled source blocks certification until it is removed or substituted; and the analyte goes onto priority surveillance for this row. No component ceiling is invented in branch (c), because no credible sovereign government publishes a total-chromium content maximum for these components in this product form, and an occupational exposure limit, an indoor-air guideline and an ambient-air standard are none of them converted into one. The post-use emission-path residue analysis identified at Step 0 is a mass-balance check on the published test, not a second ceiling, because a residue recovered after use has lost the volatile mass that made up most of the emitting material, so its concentration carries a denominator far smaller than the published basis and is not comparable to it. It is dispositioned in three steps, so that it too carries an outcome. First, the published total-chromium screen of 600 ug/kg governs certification on the emitting material as sampled above: an emitting-material total-chromium result reaching half the screen (300 ug/kg) orders reflex hexavalent-chromium speciation and the Cr-VI cell's zero-tolerance disposition then governs the lot, whatever the post-use emission-path residue analysis identified at Step 0 shows. Second, the residue result is reconciled to the published basis: the measured residue concentration is multiplied by the recovered residue mass and divided by the mass of emitting material consumed, to give the mass of element retained per kilogram of the emitting material as supplied, and the balance against the as-supplied content is the fraction that left the article; where that reconciliation cannot be closed within the combined expanded uncertainty of the two determinations, or where the residue concentration implies a retention inconsistent with the declared formulation, the published test is treated as having been run on an unrepresentative sample, the lot is held, re-sampled from a fresh unit under the Step 0 conditions of use and re-tested, and a confirmed unrepresentative published test fails the lot. Third, the result itself is dispositioned under the control-and-escalation rule stated above, with CAPA and raw-material traceback on an unexplained step change and a block on certification where an uncontrolled source is confirmed.
